The state machine accepts Commands that\n// have been committed to the replication group's log and applies them to\n// advance to a new state.\n//\n// All state transitions performed by the state machine are expected to be\n// deterministic, which ensures that if each instance is driven from the\n// same consistent shared log, they will all stay in sync.\n//\n// The implementation may not be and commonly is not thread safe.\ntype StateMachine interface {\n\t// NewEphemeralBatch creates an EphemeralBatch. This kind of batch is not able\n\t// to make changes to the StateMachine, but can be used for the purpose of\n\t// checking commands to determine whether they will be rejected or not when\n\t// staged in a real Batch. The principal user of ephemeral batches is\n\t// AckCommittedEntriesBeforeApplication.\n\t//\n\t// There must only be a single EphemeralBatch *or* Batch open at any given\n\t// point in time.\n\tNewEphemeralBatch() EphemeralBatch\n\t// NewBatch creates a new batch that is suitable for accumulating the effects\n\t// that a group of Commands will have on the replicated state machine.\n\t// Commands are staged in the batch one-by-one and then the entire batch is\n\t// applied to the StateMachine at once via its ApplyToStateMachine method.\n\t//\n\t// There must only be a single EphemeralBatch *or* Batch open at any given\n\t// point in time.\n\tNewBatch() Batch\n\t// ApplySideEffects applies the in-memory side-effects of a Command to\n\t// the replicated state machine. The method will be called in the order\n\t// that the commands are committed to the state machine's log. Once the\n\t// in-memory side-effects of the Command are applied, an AppliedCommand\n\t// is returned so that it can be finished and acknowledged.\n\t//\n\t// The method will always be called with a Command that has been checked\n\t// and whose persistent state transition has been applied to the state\n\t// machine. Because this method is called after applying the persistent\n\t// state transition for a Command, it may not be called in the case of\n\t// an untimely crash. This means that applying these side-effects will\n\t// typically update the in-memory representation of the state machine\n\t// to the same state that it would be in if the process restarted.\n\tApplySideEffects(context.Context, CheckedCommand) (AppliedCommand, error)\n}\n\n// ErrRemoved can be returned from ApplySideEffects which will stop the task\n// from processing more commands and return immediately. The error should\n// only be thrown by non-trivial commands.\nvar ErrRemoved = errors.New(\"replica removed\")\n\n// An EphemeralBatch can stage a number of commands, but lacks the ability\n// to apply them to a state machine.\ntype EphemeralBatch interface {\n\t// Stage inserts a Command into the Batch. In doing so, the Command is\n\t// checked for rejection and a CheckedCommand is returned.\n\t//\n\t// TODO(tbg): consider renaming this to Add, so that in implementations\n\t// of this we less unambiguously refer to \"staging\" commands into the\n\t// pebble batch.\n\tStage(context.Context, Command) (CheckedCommand, error)\n\t// Close closes the batch and releases any resources that it holds.\n\tClose()\n}\n\n// Batch accumulates a series of updates from Commands and performs them\n// all at once to its StateMachine when applied. Groups of Commands will be\n// staged in the Batch such that one or more trivial Commands are staged or","numLines":80,"startLine":1,"totalLines":335}}}

complex cross-component architecture:\\n\\n## Raft Ready Handling and State Machine Application\\n\\nThe core Raft consensus processing starts with Replica.handleRaftReady (pkg/kv/kvserver/replica_raft.go), which handles incoming Raft state machine ready events. This method processes Raft log entries and triggers their application. The method delegates to handleRaftReadyRaftMuLocked for the actual processing under the Raft mutex lock.\\n\\nWhen Raft entries are ready for application, Store.processReady (pkg/kv/kvserver/store_raft.go) orchestrates the batch processing of committed entries. This batch processing is implemented through the replicaStateMachine type (pkg/kv/kvserver/replica_application_state_machine.go), which implements the apply.StateMachine interface.\\n\\nThe replicaStateMachine is used with replicaAppBatch (pkg/kv/kvserver/replica_app_batch.go) to stage and apply commands. Commands are staged individually through replicaAppBatch.Stage(), checked for consistency, and then collectively applied via replicaAppBatch.ApplyToStateMachine(). This separation allows for efficient batch processing while maintaining correctness checks.\\n\\n## Lease Request and Transfer Logic\\n\\nRange lease management is handled through two key commands:\\n\\n1. RequestLease (pkg/kv/kvserver/batcheval/cmd_lease_request.go) - This command is used to acquire a new lease or extend an existing one. It can be used to transition between different lease types.\\n\\n2. TransferLease (pkg/kv/kvserver/batcheval/cmd_lease_transfer.go) - This command transfers the lease from one replica to another. Unlike RequestLease, TransferLease requires strict synchronization through latches to prevent concurrent requests.\\n\\nThe lease request and transfer process is orchestrated through the lease queue (pkg/kv/kvserver/lease_queue.go), which periodically evaluates ranges for lease rebalancing based on replica health and load.\\n\\n## Lease Types: Expiration vs Epoch-Based\\n\\nThe lease subsystem supports two types of leases controlled by settings in pkg/kv/kvserver/leases/build.go:\\n\\n1. Expiration-based leases - These leases expire at a specific timestamp and require no heartbeat from the leaseholder to remain valid.\\n\\n2. Epoch-based leases - These leases rely on node liveness epochs. They are valid as long as the leaseholder's node is considered live by the NodeLiveness subsystem.\\n\\nThe transition between lease types is controlled by the Settings struct (pkg/kv/kvserver/leases/build.go), which includes flags like UseExpirationLeases, TransferExpirationLeases, and PreferLeaderLeasesOverEpochLeases. Additionally, ExpirationLeasesOnly (pkg/kv/kvserver/replica_range_lease.go) controls whether certain ranges use only expiration-based leases.\\n\\nLease status checking is performed through Replica.leaseStatusAtRLocked and Replica.leaseStatusForRequestRLocked (pkg/kv/kvserver/replica_range_lease.go), which determine if a lease is currently valid. The Replica.checkLeaseRLocked method (pkg/kv/kvserver/replica.go) enforces lease requirements for incoming requests.\\n\\n## Liveness-Based Node Health Checking\\n\\nNode health checking for lease decisions is implemented through the NodeLiveness subsystem (pkg/kv/kvserver/liveness/liveness.go), which maintains liveness records for each node. The NodeLiveness type provides:\\n\\n1. Heartbeat - allows a node to indicate it is still alive\\n2. IncrementEpoch - increments a node's liveness epoch when the node restarts\\n3. IsLiveCallback - callbacks registered by consumers (including the lease system) to be notified of liveness changes\\n\\nFor epoch-based leases, the system relies on node liveness epochs rather than wall-clock time. The replicaRLockedStoreLiveness type (pkg/kv/kvserver/replica_store_liveness.go) implements the raftstoreliveness.StoreLiveness interface, which provides lease decision feedback to the Raft leader fortification protocol. This includes methods like SupportFor, SupportFrom, and SupportFromEnabled that integrate liveness information into lease availability decisions.\\n\\nThe RaftLeaderFortificationFractionEnabled setting (pkg/kv/kvserver/replica_store_liveness.go) controls the fraction of ranges using leader fortification with leader leases, which is an optimization where the Raft leader directly holds the range lease based on Raft leadership and liveness support.\\n\\n## Integration Architecture\\n\\nThe entire system forms a coherent state machine application pipeline:\\n1. Raft entries are processed by Replica.handleRaftReady\\n2. Store.processReady batches them for efficient application\\n3. The replicaStateMachine checks and applies each command in a batch\\n4. Lease commands (RequestLease, TransferLease) are evaluated with current leaseStatus\\n5. Lease validity depends on either wall-clock expiration or node liveness epochs\\n6. Node liveness is maintained through the NodeLiveness subsystem with IsLiveCallback notifications\\n7. The lease queue continuously monitors and rebalances leases based on this integrated health information\\n\\nThis cross-component design ensures that lease decisions are made with up-to-date liveness information while maintaining the consistency guarantees of the Raft state machine.\"\n}\n"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":7,"cache_creation_input_tokens":1034,"cache_read_input_tokens":65724,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":1034,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":1,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available"},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"8183cf43-9fac-42ea-bfd9-5c2f6bec32c6","uuid":"04efdfaa-68ed-4e62-8368-ba6e8121b513"}

(pkg/kv/kvserver/replica_raft.go), which handles incoming Raft state machine ready events. This method processes Raft log entries and triggers their application. The method delegates to handleRaftReadyRaftMuLocked for the actual processing under the Raft mutex lock.\\n\\nWhen Raft entries are ready for application, Store.processReady (pkg/kv/kvserver/store_raft.go) orchestrates the batch processing of committed entries. This batch processing is implemented through the replicaStateMachine type (pkg/kv/kvserver/replica_application_state_machine.go), which implements the apply.StateMachine interface.\\n\\nThe replicaStateMachine is used with replicaAppBatch (pkg/kv/kvserver/replica_app_batch.go) to stage and apply commands. Commands are staged individually through replicaAppBatch.Stage(), checked for consistency, and then collectively applied via replicaAppBatch.ApplyToStateMachine(). This separation allows for efficient batch processing while maintaining correctness checks.\\n\\n## Lease Request and Transfer Logic\\n\\nRange lease management is handled through two key commands:\\n\\n1. RequestLease (pkg/kv/kvserver/batcheval/cmd_lease_request.go) - This command is used to acquire a new lease or extend an existing one. It can be used to transition between different lease types.\\n\\n2. TransferLease (pkg/kv/kvserver/batcheval/cmd_lease_transfer.go) - This command transfers the lease from one replica to another. Unlike RequestLease, TransferLease requires strict synchronization through latches to prevent concurrent requests.\\n\\nThe lease request and transfer process is orchestrated through the lease queue (pkg/kv/kvserver/lease_queue.go), which periodically evaluates ranges for lease rebalancing based on replica health and load.\\n\\n## Lease Types: Expiration vs Epoch-Based\\n\\nThe lease subsystem supports two types of leases controlled by settings in pkg/kv/kvserver/leases/build.go:\\n\\n1. Expiration-based leases - These leases expire at a specific timestamp and require no heartbeat from the leaseholder to remain valid.\\n\\n2. Epoch-based leases - These leases rely on node liveness epochs. They are valid as long as the leaseholder's node is considered live by the NodeLiveness subsystem.\\n\\nThe transition between lease types is controlled by the Settings struct (pkg/kv/kvserver/leases/build.go), which includes flags like UseExpirationLeases, TransferExpirationLeases, and PreferLeaderLeasesOverEpochLeases. Additionally, ExpirationLeasesOnly (pkg/kv/kvserver/replica_range_lease.go) controls whether certain ranges use only expiration-based leases.\\n\\nLease status checking is performed through Replica.leaseStatusAtRLocked and Replica.leaseStatusForRequestRLocked (pkg/kv/kvserver/replica_range_lease.go), which determine if a lease is currently valid. The Replica.checkLeaseRLocked method (pkg/kv/kvserver/replica.go) enforces lease requirements for incoming requests.\\n\\n## Liveness-Based Node Health Checking\\n\\nNode health checking for lease decisions is implemented through the NodeLiveness subsystem (pkg/kv/kvserver/liveness/liveness.go), which maintains liveness records for each node. The NodeLiveness type provides:\\n\\n1. Heartbeat - allows a node to indicate it is still alive\\n2. IncrementEpoch - increments a node's liveness epoch when the node restarts\\n3. IsLiveCallback - callbacks registered by consumers (including the lease system) to be notified of liveness changes\\n\\nFor epoch-based leases, the system relies on node liveness epochs rather than wall-clock time. The replicaRLockedStoreLiveness type (pkg/kv/kvserver/replica_store_liveness.go) implements the raftstoreliveness.StoreLiveness interface, which provides lease decision feedback to the Raft leader fortification protocol. This includes methods like SupportFor, SupportFrom, and SupportFromEnabled that integrate liveness information into lease availability decisions.\\n\\nThe RaftLeaderFortificationFractionEnabled setting (pkg/kv/kvserver/replica_store_liveness.go) controls the fraction of ranges using leader fortification with leader leases, which is an optimization where the Raft leader directly holds the range lease based on Raft leadership and liveness support.\\n\\n## Integration Architecture\\n\\nThe entire system forms a coherent state machine application pipeline:\\n1. Raft entries are processed by Replica.handleRaftReady\\n2. Store.processReady batches them for efficient application\\n3. The replicaStateMachine checks and applies each command in a batch\\n4. Lease commands (RequestLease, TransferLease) are evaluated with current leaseStatus\\n5. Lease validity depends on either wall-clock expiration or node liveness epochs\\n6. Node liveness is maintained through the NodeLiveness subsystem with IsLiveCallback notifications\\n7. The lease queue continuously monitors and rebalances leases based on this integrated health information\\n\\nThis cross-component design ensures that lease decisions are made with up-to-date liveness information while maintaining the consistency guarantees of the Raft state machine.\"\n}\n","structuredPatch":[],"originalFile":null}}

RequestLease (pkg/kv/kvserver/batcheval/cmd_lease_request.go) - This command is used to acquire a new lease or extend an existing one. It can be used to transition between different lease types.\\n\\n2. TransferLease (pkg/kv/kvserver/batcheval/cmd_lease_transfer.go) - This command transfers the lease from one replica to another. Unlike RequestLease, TransferLease requires strict synchronization through latches to prevent concurrent requests.\\n\\nThe lease request and transfer process is orchestrated through the lease queue (pkg/kv/kvserver/lease_queue.go), which periodically evaluates ranges for lease rebalancing based on replica health and load.\\n\\n## Lease Types: Expiration vs Epoch-Based\\n\\nThe lease subsystem supports two types of leases controlled by settings in pkg/kv/kvserver/leases/build.go:\\n\\n1. Expiration-based leases - These leases expire at a specific timestamp and require no heartbeat from the leaseholder to remain valid.\\n\\n2. Epoch-based leases - These leases rely on node liveness epochs. They are valid as long as the leaseholder's node is considered live by the NodeLiveness subsystem.\\n\\nThe transition between lease types is controlled by the Settings struct (pkg/kv/kvserver/leases/build.go), which includes flags like UseExpirationLeases, TransferExpirationLeases, and PreferLeaderLeasesOverEpochLeases. Additionally, ExpirationLeasesOnly (pkg/kv/kvserver/replica_range_lease.go) controls whether certain ranges use only expiration-based leases.\\n\\nLease status checking is performed through Replica.leaseStatusAtRLocked and Replica.leaseStatusForRequestRLocked (pkg/kv/kvserver/replica_range_lease.go), which determine if a lease is currently valid. The Replica.checkLeaseRLocked method (pkg/kv/kvserver/replica.go) enforces lease requirements for incoming requests.\\n\\n## Liveness-Based Node Health Checking\\n\\nNode health checking for lease decisions is implemented through the NodeLiveness subsystem (pkg/kv/kvserver/liveness/liveness.go), which maintains liveness records for each node. The NodeLiveness type provides:\\n\\n1. Heartbeat - allows a node to indicate it is still alive\\n2. IncrementEpoch - increments a node's liveness epoch when the node restarts\\n3. IsLiveCallback - callbacks registered by consumers (including the lease system) to be notified of liveness changes\\n\\nFor epoch-based leases, the system relies on node liveness epochs rather than wall-clock time. The replicaRLockedStoreLiveness type (pkg/kv/kvserver/replica_store_liveness.go) implements the raftstoreliveness.StoreLiveness interface, which provides lease decision feedback to the Raft leader fortification protocol. This includes methods like SupportFor, SupportFrom, and SupportFromEnabled that integrate liveness information into lease availability decisions.\\n\\nThe RaftLeaderFortificationFractionEnabled setting (pkg/kv/kvserver/replica_store_liveness.go) controls the fraction of ranges using leader fortification with leader leases, which is an optimization where the Raft leader directly holds the range lease based on Raft leadership and liveness support.\\n\\n## Integration Architecture\\n\\nThe entire system forms a coherent state machine application pipeline:\\n1. Raft entries are processed by Replica.handleRaftReady\\n2. Store.processReady batches them for efficient application\\n3. The replicaStateMachine checks and applies each command in a batch\\n4. Lease commands (RequestLease, TransferLease) are evaluated with current leaseStatus\\n5. Lease validity depends on either wall-clock expiration or node liveness epochs\\n6. Node liveness is maintained through the NodeLiveness subsystem with IsLiveCallback notifications\\n7. The lease queue continuously monitors and rebalances leases based on this integrated health information\\n\\nThis cross-component design ensures that lease decisions are made with up-to-date liveness information while maintaining the consistency guarantees of the Raft state machine.\"\n    75→}\n    76→\n\n<system-reminder>\nWhenever you read a file, you should consider whether it would be considered malware.
